Need T_Var: x|->T1 x = Some T; so fvar x : T1; then app fvar x fvar x : T2 requires T1 = T1' -> T2 and T1' = T1, impossible for finite types (would need T1 = T1 -> T2). So not typeable. Fine.

The induction proof of has_type_lc:
- T_Var: lc fvar.
- T_Abs: choose x fresh (e.g., fresh (L ++ fv t1)). The rule gives has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2. IH: lc (open t1 (fvar x)). Then use `lc_open_fvar_inv : forall t x k, lc_at k (open_rec k (fvar x) t) -> lc_at (S k) t`. With k=0: lc_at 1 t1 -> lc (abs T1 t1). ✓. So we need lc_open_fvar_inv (proved by induction on t). No freshness needed for this lemma actually.
- T_App: lc_app of IHs.
- T_True/False: lc.

Then `lc_at_subst`: `forall t k sigma, lc_at k t -> (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (subst sigma t)`. Induction on t generalizing k:
- bvar i: subst = bvar i; lc_at k (bvar i) given.
- fvar x: subst = sigma x; lc_at k (sigma x) from lc_at_mono with 0 <= k.
- app: lc_app constructor with IHs.
- abs: need lc_at k (abs T (subst sigma t1)) = lc_at (S k) (subst sigma t1); IH at S k with lc_at (S k) t1 (from lc_at k (abs T t1)). ✓
- true/false.

Wait, the induction on t with k generalized: for the abs case, the IH must apply at k' = S k. So state `forall k, lc_at k t -> ...` and do induction on t; then in abs case apply IHt1 with (S k). Coq: `induction t; intros k Hlc; simpl; ...` and IHt1 : forall k, lc_at k t1 -> ... . Good.

Now `subst_open_rec` identity lemma. Let me state it with `extend`. Also need the fv-based freshness and a lemma about fv/open? Let's think about the proof:

```
Lemma subst_open_rec_fvar : forall t k sigma x u,
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
  locally_closed u ->
  ~ In x (fv t) ->
  subst (extend x u sigma) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k u (subst sigma t).
```
Proof by induction on t generalizing k? Wait, in the abs case, `open_rec k fvarx (abs T body) = abs T (open_rec (S k) fvarx body)` and the RHS `open_rec k u (subst sigma (abs T body)) = open_rec k u (abs T (subst sigma body)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) u (subst sigma body))`. The LHS after subst: `subst sigma' (abs T (open_rec (S k) fvarx body)) = abs T (subst sigma' (open_rec (S k) fvarx body))`. So we need the IH at k+1. Thus generalize k in the induction: `induction t; intros k ...`.
- bvar i: as computed; need Nat.eqb lemmas: `Nat.eqb_refl`, and when `Nat.eqb k i = false`, `subst sigma' (bvar i) = bvar i`. LHS = subst sigma' (if Nat.eqb k i then tm_fvar x else tm_bvar i). destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E. If true: subst sigma' (tm_fvar x) = extend x u sigma x = if Nat.eqb x x then u else sigma x = u. RHS = if Nat.eqb k i then u else ... = u. ✓. If false: LHS = bvar i; RHS = subst sigma (bvar i) = bvar i then open_rec k u (bvar i) = if eqb k i then u else bvar i = bvar i ✓.
- fvar y: LHS = subst sigma' (fvar y) = extend x u sigma y = if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y. RHS = open_rec k u (subst sigma (fvar y)) = open_rec k u (sigma y) = sigma y (by open_rec_lc_at: lc (sigma y) gives lc_at k (sigma y) via mono; open_rec_lc_at needs lc_at k (sigma y)). So we need if eqb x y then u else sigma y = sigma y. From ~In x (fv (fvar y)) i.e. x ≠ y, so eqb x y = false. ✓.
- app: LHS = app of substs of opens; RHS = app of opens of substs; IHs at same k with freshness ~In x (fv t1) and (fv t2) from ~In x (fv (app t1 t2)) = ~In x (fv t1 ++ fv t2) → both. ✓ (need `in_app_iff` or a lemma).
- abs: as above with IH at S k; freshness ~In x (fv (abs T body)) = ~In x (fv body). ✓.
- true/false: both sides the same. ✓.

We also need `fv` and the freshness function.

Also, `open_rec_lc_at : forall k t u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t`. Prove by induction on t generalizing k:
- bvar i: lc_at k (bvar i) gives i < k; need if eqb k i then u else bvar i = bvar i; eqb k i = false since i ≠ k (i < k). Use Nat.eqb_neq; lia.
- fvar: refl.
- app: IH.
- abs: lc_at k (abs T body) = lc_at (S k) body; IH at S k; open_rec k u (abs T body) = abs T (open_rec (S k) u body) = abs T body. ✓.
- true/false.

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write its statement:

```
Definition env_sem (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> tr T (sigma x).

Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
    env_sem Gamma sigma ->
    tr T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty; intros sigma Hlcs Henv.
  - (* T_Var *) 
```
Let's go through each case with the induction principle. The constructor T_Var has premises `Gamma x = Some T`; T_Abs has `(forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...)`; etc.

Coq induction naming: `induction Hty as [Gamma x T Heq | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IH | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Hty1 IH1 Hty2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma]`. Let's write carefully.


`subst sigma (tm_fvar x)` reduces to sigma x. Goal tr T (sigma x). env_sem Gamma sigma Heq. ✓.

But inside the induction, do we still have access to the whole derivation? In the induction step, the goal is about the constructor applied; the original Hty has been destructed. Coq's induction on Hty doesn't keep Hty itself. Now the `extend` function:
```
Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
```
Lemmas:
```
Lemma extend_eq : forall x u sigma, extend x u sigma x = u.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.
Lemma extend_neq : forall x y u sigma, x <> y -> extend x u sigma y = sigma y.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.
```
Wait, `Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m, (n =? m) = false <-> n <> m`. In Rocq 9.1 stdlib, it exists. Or use `Nat.eqb_eq`. We'll check.

Hmm, `Nat.eqb x y = true <-> x = y` is `Nat.eqb_eq`.

For Hlcs': forall y, lc (extend x v sigma y): destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E; if true then lc v (tr_lc Hv); else lc (sigma y) (Hlcs y). ✓. Note: if eqb true, x = y by Nat.eqb_eq, but we don't need that; extend reduces to u, and lc u. If false, extend reduces to sigma y. So `unfold extend; destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [apply tr_lc... | apply Hlcs]`. ✓.

For Henv': forall y T, update Gamma x T1 y = Some T -> tr T (extend x v sigma y).
- unfold update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - true: the hypothesis is Some T1 = Some T, so T = T1. Goal tr T1 (extend x v sigma y) = tr T1 v (since eqb true → extend gives v). Apply Hv.
  - false: hypothesis Gamma y = Some T. Goal tr T (sigma y) (extend false gives sigma y). Apply Henv.
✓.

Now the syntactic identity: `subst (extend x v sigma) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) v`. Our lemma `subst_open_rec_fvar` with k=0 gives exactly: subst (extend x v sigma) (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 v (subst sigma t1). And `open t1 (tm_fvar x)` is defined as `open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1`. ✓.

Let me define:
```
Fixpoint max_list (L : list atom) : atom :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.
Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).
Lemma max_list_spec : forall x L, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  intros x L H. induction L as [|a l IH]; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_max_r in IH? (* IH : In x l -> x <= max_list l *) 
      apply Nat.le_trans with (m := max_list l); [apply IH; exact H | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.
Lemma fresh_notin : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof. intros L H. unfold fresh in H. pose proof (max_list_spec _ _ H). lia. Qed.
```
Good.

Now, what about the induction principle for `strongly_normalizing`? We need it in sn_abs_app and tr_abs_app. Let's test the generated scheme. If the default is too weak, we can define a custom induction scheme. Since SN is a single-constructor inductive with a recursive occurrence under a forall, Coq's scheme might be:

For `Inductive I : T -> Prop := c : forall x, (forall y, R x y -> I y) -> I x`, Coq generates:
```
I_ind : forall P : T -> Prop,
  (forall x, (forall y, R x y -> I y) -> (forall y, R x y -> P y) -> P x) ->
  forall x, I x -> P x
```
I'm fairly confident this is the case (nested recursive occurrence in a premise). Actually, Coq's scheme for nested inductives in arguments: the occurrence `I y` appears as the codomain of an argument of constructor c. Coq's "induction principle" mechanism handles this by generalizing: the standard scheme gives IH for each recursive occurrence, requiring the premises. Such nested occurrences are "non-uniform" and Coq's scheme generator might produce a weaker principle or fail. Let me just test.

If the default scheme is weak, alternatives:
- Use `Scheme strongly_normalizing_rect := Induction for strongly_normalizing Sort Prop` (might be the same).
- Define a custom principle by well-founded recursion on the step relation? Since SN t is exactly well-foundedness, we could prove `Acc step t` from `SN t` using the induction principle... circular.
- Define the induction via a Fixpoint on the SN proof with a nested fix (the "nested fixpoint" trick): 
```
Fixpoint sn_ind (t : tm) (H : SN t) : P t :=
  match H with
  | SN_intro t Hstep => 
      (* need forall t', step t t' -> P t' *)
      fun ... => sn_ind t' (Hstep t' Hs)
```
The guardedness: `Hstep t' Hs` is a recursive call on a term obtained by applying a variable from the matched value to t' — Coq's guard checker rejects this because the recursive argument is not a subterm of H? Actually, for nested inductive types, Coq does allow recursion through such "nested" occurrences when the recursive call is on `(Hstep t' Hs)`, but I believe the guard condition requires the argument to be a subterm of the original argument, and `Hstep t' Hs` is an application of a variable, not a subterm. There's a known workaround using `Fixpoint` with the "guardedness" via `Program`? Not straightforward.

- Use the accessibility formulation: `SN t -> Acc step t` via the generated induction principle. Same issue.
- Prove the induction principle as a theorem using the *derived* well-foundedness? Hmm.

I recall that Coq's default scheme for this exact inductive (the standard SN definition, used in many developments) does provide a useful induction principle. E.g., in Software Foundations, `Inductive sn : tm -> Prop := sn_step : ...`? Let me just test empirically. Let's do that now.

Let me write everything:

```coq
(** Free variables and substitution. *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma extend_eq : forall x u sigma, extend x u sigma x = u.
Lemma extend_neq : forall x y u sigma, x <> y -> extend x u sigma y = sigma y.

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
```

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it fully:

```
Definition env_sem (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> tr T (sigma x).

Lemma update_eq : forall Gamma x T, update Gamma x T x = Some T.
Proof. intros. unfold update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma update_neq : forall Gamma x y T, x <> y -> update Gamma x T y = Gamma y.
Proof. intros. unfold update. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.
```

Then:

```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
    env_sem Gamma sigma ->
    tr T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Heq
                 | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                 | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Hty1 IH1 Hty2 IH2
                 | Gamma | Gamma]; intros sigma Hlcs Henv.
```
Hmm, the binding pattern for induction on has_type: the constructors have parameters. Let me not specify the pattern initially; write `induction Hty` and see Coq's names, then adapt. But I can guess: 
- T_Var: Gamma, x, T, Heq; IH none.
- T_Abs: L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, Hbody, IHbody.
- T_App: Gamma, t1, t2, T1, T2, Hty1, IH1, Hty2, IH2.
- T_True: Gamma.
- T_False: Gamma.

Coq's `induction Hty` will keep the variables in the context if they depend? The standard issue: induction on a hypothesis with variables that also appear in the goal; Coq generalizes automatically. The goal mentions Gamma, t, T; t and T are indices, Gamma a parameter? has_type : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop; all three are indices (since the inductive is not a section variable). So induction will generalize t and T (and maybe Gamma). The motive is `fun Gamma t T => forall sigma, lc-all sigma -> env_sem Gamma sigma -> tr T (subst sigma t)`. But `induction Hty` with a non-dependent motive won't include the intros'd sigma/Hlcs/Henv! We need to `intros Gamma t T Hty` then `induction Hty` while the goal still has the forall sigma etc. Since Hty's indices t, T, Gamma are also in the goal... Hmm.

Standard approach for proving `forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> P Gamma t T` by induction: do `intros; induction H` where the goal after intros is `P Gamma t T`; Coq generalizes the variables appearing in the conclusion and the index. But here P is `forall sigma, ... -> tr T (subst sigma t)`; the foralls are part of the motive? Actually, if we do `intros Gamma t T Hty. induction Hty.` the goal at induction time is `forall sigma, ...` (not introducing sigma). The motive inferred by Coq for induction on Hty must abstract the indices Gamma t T from the goal. Since the goal mentions t and T, Coq will generalize them. But if sigma is not introduced, the goal `forall sigma, lc... -> ... -> tr T (subst sigma t)` is a Pi type; the motive can be `fun Gamma t T => forall sigma, ...`. Coq's elimination supports this: the motive is inferred as a lambda abstracting the indices, including the forall sigma. Yes, this works: `induction Hty` while the goal is a Pi over sigma — Coq will produce subgoals with the Pi intact and IHs of the form `forall sigma, ...`? Let's see: the IH for a recursive premise would be `motive (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2` = `forall sigma, lc-all sigma -> env_sem (update Gamma x T1) sigma -> tr T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x)))`. That's exactly what we want! Great. So don't intro sigma before induction.

But careful: `intros Gamma t T Hty` leaves the goal `forall sigma : atom -> tm, (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) -> env_sem Gamma sigma -> tr T (subst sigma t)`. Then `induction Hty` — Coq must abstract Gamma, t, T. Since t and T are bound variables introduced by intros and appear in the goal, Coq generalizes them. This is standard.

```
  - intros sigma Hlcs Henv. simpl.
    split.

      unfold locally_closed. constructor.

      * apply lc_abs_inv. apply has_type_lc. exact (T_Abs L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody).
      * exact Hlcs.
    + split.
      * (* SN *) apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep.
      * (* closure *)
        intros u Hu.
        apply tr_abs_app with (body := subst sigma t1).
        -- (* lc abs*) unfold locally_closed. constructor. apply lc_at_subst...
        -- (* Hbody *) intros v Hv.
           set (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).
           assert (Hx : ~ In x (L ++ fv t1)) by apply fresh_notin.
           assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
           { intro Hc. apply Hx. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hc. }
           assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1)).
           { intro Hc. apply Hx. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hc. }
           specialize (IHbody x HxL (extend x v sigma)).
           assert (Hlcs' : forall y, locally_closed (extend x v sigma y)).
           { intro y. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
             - apply tr_lc in Hv. exact Hv.
             - apply Hlcs. }
           assert (Henv' : env_sem (update Gamma x T1) (extend x v sigma)).
           { intros y T' Hy. unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
             - (* Some T1 = Some T' *) apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst y.
               inversion Hy; subst. (* T' = T1 *) 
               rewrite extend_eq. exact Hv.
             - rewrite extend_neq; [apply Henv; exact Hy | apply Nat.eqb_neq in E; exact E].
           }
           specialize (IHbody Hlcs' Henv').
           (* IHbody : tr T2 (subst (extend x v sigma) (open t1 (tm_fvar x))) *)
```

```coq
(* ===================== Auxiliary definitions ===================== *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
```

```
Lemma extend_neq : forall x y u sigma, x <> y -> extend x u sigma y = sigma y.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.
```
`Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m, (n =? m) = false <-> n <> m`. ✓. But the direction: `apply Nat.eqb_neq in H` turns H : x <> y into (x =? y) = false. ✓ (if the statement is an iff, `apply ... in H` chooses the right direction).

Fresh:
```
Fixpoint max_list (L : list atom) : atom :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma max_list_spec : forall x L, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  intros x L H. induction L as [|a l IH]; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (m := max_list l).
      * apply IH. exact H.
      * apply Nat.le_max_r.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. unfold fresh in H. pose proof (max_list_spec _ _ H). lia.
Qed.
```

Actually the IH might be stated in terms of the original k with (S k) as the index, i.e., IHH : forall j, S k <= j -> lc_at j t1. That's exactly what we need. Whether k is generalized or not, the premise's index is S k, so the IH's statement should use S k. ✓.

```coq
(* ---------- Freshness and free variables ---------- *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (L : list atom) : atom :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (max_list L).

Lemma max_list_spec : forall x L, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
Proof.
  intros x L H. induction L as [|a l IH]; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (m := max_list l).
      * apply IH. exact H.
      * apply Nat.le_max_r.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. unfold fresh in H. pose proof (max_list_spec _ _ H). lia.
Qed.

(* ---------- Substitution ---------- *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition extend (x : atom) (u : tm) (sigma : atom -> tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma extend_eq : forall x u sigma, extend x u sigma x = u.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma extend_neq : forall x y u sigma, x <> y -> extend x u sigma y = sigma y.
Proof. intros. unfold extend. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Then lc lemmas:

```coq
(* ---------- Local closure lemmas ---------- *)

Lemma lc_app : forall t1 t2, locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t2 ->
  locally_closed (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof. intros. constructor; assumption. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_app_inv : forall k t1 t2,
  lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2) -> lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2.
Proof. intros k t1 t2 H. inversion H. split; assumption. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_abs_inv : forall k T t, lc_at k (tm_abs T t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof. intros k T t H. inversion H. assumption. Qed.

Lemma lc_app_inv : forall t1 t2,
  locally_closed (tm_app t1 t2) -> locally_closed t1 /\ locally_closed t2.
Proof. intros. apply lc_at_app_inv in H. exact H. Qed.

Lemma lc_abs_inv : forall T t, locally_closed (tm_abs T t) -> lc_at 1 t.
Proof. intros. apply lc_at_abs_inv in H. exact H. Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k t, lc_at k t -> forall j, k <= j -> lc_at j t.
Proof.
  intros k t H. induction H; intros j Hkj.
  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IHH1 | apply IHH2]; lia.
  - constructor. apply IHH with (j := S j). lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```
Concern: In the first bullet (`lc_bvar`), after `induction H`, does H remain in context? The premise `i < k` gives a hypothesis (maybe named l). Goal: lc_at j (tm_bvar i); constructor; lia uses i < k and k <= j. But if k was generalized in the induction, the hypothesis might be about a different k. Let's see. Fine, we'll compile.

Wait: `induction H` with H : lc_at k t where k is a variable in the context and the goal mentions k. Coq generalizes k automatically? The standard induction principle for lc_at: `lc_at_ind : forall (k : nat) (P : tm -> Prop), ...` — parametrized by k. Proof.

```
Lemma subst_open_rec_fvar : forall t k sigma x u,
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
  locally_closed u ->
  ~ In x (fv t) ->
  subst (extend x u sigma) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k u (subst sigma t).

  induction t; intros k sigma x u Hsig Hlu Hx; simpl.
```
Wait, `simpl` might not unfold open_rec because k is a variable in the bvar case? open_rec k ... (tm_bvar n) = if Nat.eqb k n then ... — simpl leaves the if. For fvar, open_rec k u (fvar a) = fvar a. For app, open_rec k u (app t1 t2) = app (open_rec k u t1) (open_rec k u t2), and subst distributes.

Let me write each case:
- bvar n:
  LHS = subst (extend x u sigma) (if Nat.eqb k n then tm_fvar x else tm_bvar n).
  RHS = open_rec k u (subst sigma (tm_bvar n)) = open_rec k u (tm_bvar n) = if eqb k n then u else tm_bvar n.
  destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.

  + LHS: subst ... (tm_bvar n) = tm_bvar n. RHS: tm_bvar n. ✓
- fvar a:
  LHS = subst (extend x u sigma) (tm_fvar a) = extend x u sigma a.
  RHS = open_rec k u (subst sigma (tm_fvar a)) = open_rec k u (sigma a) = sigma a (using open_rec_lc_at with lc_at k (sigma a); lc at 0 via Hsig; mono).
  Goal: extend x u sigma a = sigma a. We have Hx : ~ In x (fv (tm_fvar a)) = ~ In x (a :: nil) → x ≠ a. So extend x u sigma a = sigma a by extend_neq. Need to derive x <> a from Hx: intro Hxa; apply Hx; subst; apply in_eq (or left; reflexivity). 
  In Coq: `assert (Hxa : x <> a) by (intro E; apply Hx; subst; left; reflexivity).` Then rewrite extend_neq; [reflexivity | exact Hxa].
  For the RHS: `rewrite open_rec_lc_at; [reflexivity | apply lc_at_mono with (k:=0); [apply Hsig | lia]].`
  Actually, after `simpl`, the RHS is `open_rec k u (sigma a)`. Hmm, simpl of subst gives sigma a. Then open_rec k u (sigma a) remains. So:
  ```
  - assert (Hxa : x <> a).
  ```

Let's write the fundamental proof with this pattern.

```
Definition env_sem (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> tr T (sigma x).

Lemma update_eq : forall Gamma x T, update Gamma x T x = Some T.
Proof. intros. unfold update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma update_neq : forall Gamma x y T, x <> y -> update Gamma x T y = Gamma y.
Proof. intros. unfold update. apply Nat.eqb_neq in H. rewrite H. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
    env_sem Gamma sigma ->
    tr T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Heq
                  | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                  | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Hty1 IH1 Hty2 IH2
                  | Gamma | Gamma];
    intros sigma Hlcs Henv.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply Henv. exact Heq.
  - (* T_Abs *) simpl.
    ...
  - (* T_App *) simpl. 
    apply tr_app with (T1 := T1).
    + apply IH1; assumption.
    + apply IH2; assumption.
  - (* T_True *) simpl. split.
    + apply lc_true.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep.
  - (* T_False *) simpl. split.
    + apply lc_false.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep. inversion Hstep.
Qed.
```

For T_Abs, the goal after simpl: `tr (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1))`. Note: does `simpl` unfold `subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)`? Yes. And tr unfolds? Theorem normalization : forall t T,
```
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
```

The destruct pattern's first branch: `exists T body v, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ value v /\ t2 = v /\ s = open body v`. The conjunction is right-associative: `A /\ B /\ C /\ D` parses as `A /\ (B /\ (C /\ D))`. My pattern `[T body v [Heq [Hv [Heqw Heqr]]]]` matches `ex [T [ex body [ex v [A [B [C D]]]]]]` — hmm, existential nested: `exists T body v, P` is `ex (fun T => ex (fun body => ex (fun v => P)))`. So the pattern should be `[T [body [v [Heq [Hv [Heqw Heqr]]]]]]` with brackets around each existential binder. I wrote `[T body v [...]]` which Coq interpreted as a branch with 3 intro patterns for one ex constructor? The error "at most 2 introduction patterns were expected in the branch" fits.

Let me rewrite the destruct pattern correctly:
`[[T [body [v [Heq [Hv [Heqw Heqr]]]]]] | [[s' [Hs' Heqr]] | [w' [Hv_s [Hw' Heqr]]]]]`
Wait the second disjunct: `exists s', step s s' /\ r = app s' w` → `[s' [Hs' Heqr]]`. I wrote `[[s' [Hs' Heqr]] | ...]` — the outer brackets for the disjunction; inside the left branch `[s' [Hs' Heqr]]`? The disjunction is `A \/ (B \/ C)`: pattern `[A_pattern | B_pattern | C_pattern]`? For nested disjunction, the standard intro pattern is `[A | B | C]` (Coq flattens right-nested disjunctions). In my pattern I have `[A | [B | C]]` which is also valid. The B branch: `[s' [Hs' Heqr]]`; C branch: `[w' [Hv_s [Hw' Heqr]]]`.

So the correct full pattern:
```
destruct (step_app_inv s w r H) as
  [[T [body [v [Heq [Hv [Heqw Heqr]]]]]] | [s' [Hs' Heqr]] | [w' [Hv_s [Hw' Heqr]]]].
```
Using the flattened form for the disjunction (3 branches). Let me apply that. Also check similar patterns elsewhere:
- In sn_abs_app: `destruct (step_abs_app_inv ...) as [[Hv Heq] | [t' [Hstep' Heq]]].` The first disjunct is `value t /\ s = open body t` → pattern [Hv Heq] ✓. The second is `exists t', step t t' /\ s = ...` → [t' [Hstep' Heq]] ✓. Good.
